   81: =head1 NAME
   82: 
   83: App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor - Extract test schemas from Perl modules
   84: 
   85: =head1 VERSION
   86: 
   87: Version 0.51
   88: 
   89: =cut
   90: 
   91: our $VERSION = '0.51';
   92: 
   93: =head1 SYNOPSIS
   94: 
   95: 	use App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor;
   96: 
   97: 	my $extractor = App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor->new(
   98: 		input_file => 'lib/MyModule.pm',
   99: 		output_dir => 'schemas/',
  100: 		verbose	=> 1,
  101: 	);
  102: 
  103: 	my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all();
  104: 
  105: =head1 DESCRIPTION
  106: 
  107: App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor analyzes Perl modules and generates
  108: structured YAML schema files suitable for automated test generation by L<App::Test::Generator>.
  109: This module employs
  110: static analysis techniques to infer parameter types, constraints, and
  111: method behaviors directly from your source code.
  112: 
  113: =head2 Analysis Methods
  114: 
  115: The extractor combines multiple analysis approaches for a comprehensive schema generation:
  116: 
  117: =over 4
  118: 
  119: =item * B<POD Documentation Analysis>
  120: 
  121: Parses embedded documentation to extract:
  122:   - Parameter names, types, and descriptions from =head2 sections
  123:   - Method signatures with positional parameters
  124:   - Return value specifications from "Returns:" sections
  125:   - Constraints (ranges, patterns, required/optional status)
  126:     (in a formal C<=head4 Input> spec, C<required =E<gt> 1> means
  127:     C<optional: 0> and an explicit C<optional> flag wins; a bare list of
  128:     C<name =E<gt> { ... }> entries with no outer braces contributes only
  129:     its top-level C<required> flags)
  130:   - Semantic type detection (email, URL, filename)
  131: 
  132: =item * B<Code Pattern Detection>
  133: 
  134: Analyzes source code using PPI to identify:
  135:   - Method signatures and parameter extraction patterns
  136:   - Type validation (ref(), isa(), blessed())
  137:   - Constraint patterns (length checks, numeric comparisons, regex matches)
  138:   - Return statement analysis and value type inference
  139:   - Object instantiation requirements and accessor methods
  140: 
  141: =item * B<Signature Analysis>
  142: 
  143: Examines method declarations for:
  144:   - Parameter names and positional information
  145:   - Instance vs. class method detection
  146:   - Method modifiers (Moose-style before/after/around)
  147:   - Various parameter declaration styles (shift, @_ assignment)
  148: 
  149: =item * B<Heuristic Inference>
  150: 
  151: Applies Perl-specific domain knowledge:
  152:   - Boolean return detection from method names (is_*, has_*, can_*)
  153:   - Common Perl idioms and coding patterns
  154:   - Context awareness (scalar vs list, wantarray usage)
  155:   - Object-oriented patterns (constructors, accessors, chaining)
  156: 
  157: =back
  158: 
  159: =head2 Generated Schema Structure
  160: 
  161: The extracted schemas follow this YAML structure:
  162: 
  163:     function: method_name
  164:     module: Package::Name
  165:     input:
  166:       param1:
  167:         type: string
  168:         min: 3
  169:         max: 50
  170:         optional: 0
  171:         position: 0
  172:       param2:
  173:         type: integer
  174:         min: 0
  175:         max: 100
  176:         optional: 1
  177:         position: 1
  178:     output:
  179:       type: boolean
  180:       value: 1
  181:     new: Package::Name # if object instantiation required
  182:     config:
  183:       test_empty: 1
  184:       test_nuls: 0
  185:       test_undef: 0
  186:       test_non_ascii: 0
  187: 
  188: =head2 Advanced Detection Capabilities
  189: 
  190: =over 4
  191: 
  192: =item * B<Accessor Method Detection>
  193: 
  194: Automatically identifies getter, setter, and combined accessor methods
  195: by analyzing common patterns like C<return $self-E<gt>{property}> and
  196: C<$self-E<gt>{property} = $value>.
  197: 
  198: =item * B<Params::Get Integration>
  199: 
  200: Recognises parameters extracted via C<Params::Get::get_params('key', \@_)>,
  201: treating the quoted key as a named parameter equivalent to a traditional
  202: C<my ($self, $key) = @_> signature.  This prevents false positives from
  203: C<--strict-pod> when the method body never declares an explicit C<$key>
  204: variable.
  205: 
  206: =item * B<Direct-Index Self Style>
  207: 
  208: Recognises C<my $self = $_[0]> as a valid method-invocant pattern.  Parameters
  209: at C<$_[1]>, C<$_[2]>, etc. are extracted as positional parameters.  Without
  210: this, the signature fallback would incorrectly pick up C<my (...) = @_> from
  211: inner closures defined in the method body and treat those variables as the
  212: outer method's parameters.
  213: 
  214: =item * B<Boolean Return Inference>
  215: 
  216: Detects boolean-returning methods through multiple signals:
  217:   - Method name patterns (is_*, has_*, can_*)
  218:   - Return patterns (consistent 1/0 returns)
  219:   - POD descriptions ("returns true on success")
  220:   - Ternary operators with boolean results
  221: 
  222: =item * B<Context Awareness>
  223: 
  224: Identifies methods that use C<wantarray> and can return different
  225: values in scalar vs list context.
  226: 
  227: =item * B<Object Lifecycle Management>
  228: 
  229: Detects instance methods requiring object instantiation and
  230: automatically adds the C<new> field to schemas.
  231: 
  232: =item * B<Enhanced Object Detection>
  233: 
  234: The extractor includes sophisticated object detection capabilities that go beyond simple instance method identification:
  235: 
  236: =over 4
  237: 
  238: =item * B<Factory Method Recognition>
  239: 
  240: Automatically identifies methods that create and return object instances, such as methods named C<create_*>, C<make_*>, C<build_*>, or C<get_*>. Factory methods are correctly classified as class methods that don't require pre-existing objects for testing.
  241: 
  242: =item * B<Singleton Pattern Detection>
  243: 
  244: Recognizes singleton patterns through multiple signals: method names like C<instance> or C<get_instance>, static variables holding instance references, lazy initialization patterns (C<$instance ||= new()>), and consistent return of the same instance variable.
  245: 
  246: =item * B<Constructor Parameter Analysis>
  247: 
  248: Examines C<new> methods to determine required and optional parameters, validation requirements, and default values. This enables test generators to provide appropriate constructor arguments when object instantiation is needed.
  249: 
  250: A constructor that rejects an empty arrayref, or calls methods on (or
  251: checks C<blessed> on) the elements of a required arrayref parameter, needs
  252: live objects that a YAML fixture cannot hold.  Its C<new> schema gets a
  253: C<skip> reason such as C<needs a non-empty arrayref of live objects in
  254: 'databases'>, and its instance methods are emitted with C<new: ~> so
  255: harnesses skip them.
  256: 
  257: =item * B<Inheritance Relationship Handling>
  258: 
  259: Detects parent classes through C<use parent>, C<use base>, and C<@ISA> declarations. Identifies when methods use C<SUPER::> calls and determines whether the current class or a parent class constructor should be used for object instantiation.
  260: 
  261: =item * B<External Object Dependency Detection>
  262: 
  263: Identifies when methods create or depend on objects from other classes, enabling proper test setup with mock objects or real dependencies.
  264: 
  265: =back
  266: 
  267: These enhancements ensure that generated test schemas accurately reflect the object-oriented structure of the code, leading to more meaningful and effective test generation.
  268: 
  269: =back
  270: 
  271: =head2 Confidence Scoring
  272: 
  273: Each generated schema includes detailed confidence assessments:
  274: 
  275: =over 4
  276: 
  277: =item * B<High Confidence>
  278: 
  279: Multiple independent analysis sources converge on consistent,
  280: well-constrained parameters with explicit validation logic and
  281: comprehensive documentation.
  282: 
  283: =item * B<Medium Confidence>
  284: 
  285: Reasonable evidence from code patterns or partial documentation,
  286: but may lack comprehensive constraints or have some ambiguities.
  287: 
  288: =item * B<Low Confidence>
  289: 
  290: Minimal evidence - primarily based on naming conventions,
  291: default assumptions, or single-source analysis.
  292: 
  293: =item * B<Very Low Confidence>
  294: 
  295: Barely any detectable signals - schema should be thoroughly
  296: reviewed before use in test generation.
  297: 
  298: =back
  299: 
  300: =head2 Use Cases
  301: 
  302: =over 4
  303: 
  304: =item * B<Automated Test Generation>
  305: 
  306: Generate comprehensive test suites with L<App::Test::Generator> using
  307: extracted schemas as input. The schemas provide the necessary structure
  308: for generating both positive and negative test cases.
  309: 
  310: =item * B<API Documentation Generation>
  311: 
  312: Supplement existing documentation with automatically inferred interface
  313: specifications, parameter requirements, and return types.
  314: 
  315: =item * B<Code Quality Assessment>
  316: 
  317: Identify methods with poor documentation, inconsistent parameter handling,
  318: or unclear interfaces that may benefit from refactoring.
  319: 
  320: =item * B<Refactoring Assistance>
  321: 
  322: Detect method dependencies, object instantiation requirements, and
  323: parameter usage patterns to inform refactoring decisions.
  324: 
  325: =item * B<Legacy Code Analysis>
  326: 
  327: Quickly understand the interface contracts of legacy Perl codebases
  328: without extensive manual code reading.
  329: 
  330: =back
  331: 
  332: =head2 Integration with Testing Ecosystem
  333: 
  334: The generated schemas are specifically designed to work with the
  335: L<App::Test::Generator> ecosystem:
  336: 
  337:     # Extract schemas from your module
  338:     my $extractor = App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor->new(...);
  339:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all();
  340: 
  341:     # Use with test generator (typically as separate steps)
  342:     # fuzz-harness-generator -r schemas/method_name.yml
  343: 
  344: =head2 Limitations and Considerations
  345: 
  346: =over 4
  347: 
  348: =item * B<Dynamic Code Patterns>
  349: 
  350: Highly dynamic code (string evals, AUTOLOAD, symbolic references)
  351: may not be fully detected by static analysis.
  352: 
  353: =item * B<Complex Validation Logic>
  354: 
  355: Sophisticated validation involving multiple parameters or external
  356: dependencies may require manual schema refinement.
  357: 
  358: =item * B<Confidence Heuristics>
  359: 
  360: Confidence scores are based on heuristics and should be reviewed
  361: by developers familiar with the codebase.
  362: 
  363: =item * B<Perl Idiom Recognition>
  364: 
  365: Some Perl-specific idioms may require custom pattern recognition
  366: beyond the built-in detectors.
  367: 
  368: =item * B<Documentation Dependency>
  369: 
  370: Analysis quality improves significantly with comprehensive POD
  371: documentation following consistent patterns.
  372: 
  373: =item * B<Multi-Package Files>
  374: 
  375: Only the first package in a file is analysed.  A file that declares
  376: additional packages, such as a small error class, is still extracted, but
  377: its schemas carry a C<skip> reason so generated harnesses are not run
  378: against it.  Perl 5.38 C<class> blocks are not detected in such a file:
  379: the C<class>/C<method> scan reads the whole source, so it runs only when
  380: the file declares at most one C<package>.
  381: 
  382: =back
  383: 
  384: =head2 Best Practices for Optimal Results
  385: 
  386: =over 4
  387: 
  388: =item * B<Comprehensive POD Documentation>
  389: 
  390: Write detailed POD with explicit parameter documentation using
  391: consistent patterns like C<$param - type (constraints), description>.
  392: 
  393: =item * B<Consistent Coding Patterns>
  394: 
  395: Use consistent parameter validation patterns and method signatures
  396: throughout your codebase.
  397: 
  398: =item * B<Schema Review Process>
  399: 
  400: Review and refine automatically generated schemas, particularly
  401: those with low confidence scores.
  402: 
  403: =item * B<Descriptive Naming>
  404: 
  405: Use descriptive method and parameter names that clearly indicate
  406: purpose and expected types.
  407: 
  408: =item * B<Progressive Enhancement>
  409: 
  410: Start with automatically generated schemas and progressively
  411: refine them based on test results and code understanding.
  412: 
  413: =back
  414: 
  415: The module is particularly valuable for large codebases where manual schema
  416: creation would be prohibitively time-consuming, and for maintaining test
  417: coverage as code evolves through continuous integration pipelines.
  418: 
  419: =head2 Advanced Type Detection
  420: 
  421: The schema extractor includes enhanced type detection capabilities that identify specialized Perl types beyond basic strings and integers.
  422: L<DateTime> and L<Time::Piece> objects are detected through isa() checks and method call patterns, while date strings (ISO 8601, YYYY-MM-DD) and UNIX timestamps are recognized through regex validation and numeric range checks.
  423: File handles and file paths are identified via I/O operations and file test operators, coderefs are detected through ref() checks and invocation patterns, and enum-like parameters are extracted from validation code including regex patterns (C</^(a|b|c)$/>), hash lookups, grep statements, and if/elsif chains.
  424: These detected types are preserved in the generated YAML schemas with appropriate semantic annotations, enabling test generators to create more accurate and meaningful test cases.
  425: 
  426: =head3 Example Advanced Type Schema
  427: 
  428: For a method like:
  429: 
  430:     sub process_event {
  431:         my ($self, $timestamp, $status, $callback) = @_;
  432:         croak unless $timestamp > 1000000000;
  433:         croak unless $status =~ /^(active|pending|complete)$/;
  434:         croak unless ref($callback) eq 'CODE';
  435:         $callback->($timestamp, $status);
  436:     }
  437: 
  438: The extractor generates:
  439: 
  440:     ---
  441:     function: process_event
  442:     module: MyModule
  443:     input:
  444:       timestamp:
  445:         type: integer
  446:         # min: 0
  447:         # max: 2147483647
  448:         position: 0
  449:         _note: Unix timestamp
  450: 	semantic: unix_timestamp
  451:       status:
  452:         type: string
  453:         enum:
  454:           - active
  455:           - pending
  456:           - complete
  457:         position: 1
  458:         _note: 'Must be one of: active, pending, complete'
  459:       callback:
  460:         type: coderef
  461:         position: 2
  462:         _note: 'CODE reference - provide sub { } in tests'
  463: 
  464: =head1 RELATIONSHIP DETECTION
  465: 
  466: The schema extractor detects relationships and dependencies between parameters,
  467: enabling more sophisticated validation and test generation.
  468: 
  469: =head2 Relationship Types
  470: 
  471: =over 4
  472: 
  473: =item * B<mutually_exclusive>
  474: 
  475: Parameters that cannot be used together.
  476: 
  477:     die if $file && $content;  # Can't specify both
  478: 
  479: Generated schema:
  480: 
  481:     relationships:
  482:       - type: mutually_exclusive
  483:         params: [file, content]
  484:         description: Cannot specify both file and content
  485: 
  486: =item * B<required_group>
  487: 
  488: At least one parameter from the group must be specified (OR logic).
  489: 
  490:     die unless $id || $name;  # Must provide one
  491: 
  492: Generated schema:
  493: 
  494:     relationships:
  495:       - type: required_group
  496:         params: [id, name]
  497:         logic: or
  498:         description: Must specify either id or name
  499: 
  500: =item * B<conditional_requirement>
  501: 
  502: If one parameter is specified, another becomes required (IF-THEN logic).
  503: 
  504:     die if $async && !$callback;  # async requires callback
  505: 
  506: Generated schema:
  507: 
  508:     relationships:
  509:       - type: conditional_requirement
  510:         if: async
  511:         then_required: callback
  512:         description: When async is specified, callback is required
  513: 
  514: =item * B<dependency>
  515: 
  516: One parameter depends on another being present.
  517: 
  518:     die "Port requires host" if $port && !$host;
  519: 
  520: Generated schema:
  521: 
  522:     relationships:
  523:       - type: dependency
  524:         param: port
  525:         requires: host
  526:         description: port requires host to be specified
  527: 
  528: =item * B<value_constraint>
  529: 
  530: Specific value requirements between parameters.
  531: 
  532:     die if $ssl && $port != 443;  # ssl requires port 443
  533: 
  534: Generated schema:
  535: 
  536:     relationships:
  537:       - type: value_constraint
  538:         if: ssl
  539:         then: port
  540:         operator: ==
  541:         value: 443
  542:         description: When ssl is specified, port must equal 443
  543: 
  544: =item * B<value_conditional>
  545: 
  546: Parameter required when another has a specific value.
  547: 
  548:     die if $mode eq 'secure' && !$key;
  549: 
  550: Generated schema:
  551: 
  552:     relationships:
  553:       - type: value_conditional
  554:         if: mode
  555:         equals: secure
  556:         then_required: key
  557:         description: When mode equals 'secure', key is required
  558: 
  559: =back
  560: 
  561: =head2 Default Value Extraction
  562: 
  563: The extractor comprehensively extracts default values from both code and POD documentation:
  564: 
  565: =head3 Code Pattern Recognition
  566: 
  567: Extracts defaults from multiple Perl idioms:
  568: 
  569: =over 4
  570: 
  571: =item * Logical OR operator: C<$param = $param || 'default'>
  572: 
  573: =item * Defined-or operator: C<$param //= 'default'>
  574: 
  575: =item * Ternary operator: C<$param = defined $param ? $param : 'default'>
  576: 
  577: =item * Unless conditional: C<$param = 'default' unless defined $param>
  578: 
  579: =item * Chained defaults: C<$param = $param || $self->{_default} || 'fallback'>
  580: 
  581: =item * Multi-line patterns: C<$param = {} unless $param>
  582: 
  583: =back
  584: 
  585: =head3 POD Pattern Recognition
  586: 
  587: Extracts defaults from documentation:
  588: 
  589: =over 4
  590: 
  591: =item * Standard format: C<Default: 'value'>
  592: 
  593: =item * Alternative format: C<Defaults to: 'value'>
  594: 
  595: =item * Inline format: C<Optional, default: 'value'>
  596: 
  597: =item * Parameter lists: C<$param - type, default 'value'>
  598: 
  599: =back
  600: 
  601: =head3 Value Processing
  602: 
  603: Properly handles:
  604: 
  605: =over 4
  606: 
  607: =item * String literals with quotes and escape sequences
  608: 
  609: =item * Numeric values (integers and floats)
  610: 
  611: =item * Boolean values (true/false converted to 1/0)
  612: 
  613: =item * Empty data structures ([] and {})
  614: 
  615: =item * Special values (undef, __PACKAGE__)
  616: 
  617: =item * Complex expressions (preserved as-is when unevaluatable)
  618: 
  619: =item * Quote operators (q{}, qq{}, qw{})
  620: 
  621: =back
  622: 
  623: =head3 Type Inference
  624: 
  625: When a parameter has a default value but no explicit type annotation,
  626: the type is automatically inferred from the default:
  627: 
  628:     $options = {}        # inferred as hashref
  629:     $items = []          # inferred as arrayref
  630:     $count = 42          # inferred as integer
  631:     $ratio = 3.14        # inferred as number
  632:     $enabled = 1         # inferred as boolean
  633: 
  634: =head2 Context-Aware Return Analysis
  635: 
  636: The extractor provides comprehensive analysis of method return behavior,
  637: including context sensitivity, error handling conventions, and method chaining patterns.
  638: 
  639: When a method's POD contains a C<=head4 Output> block in
  640: L<Params::Validate::Strict> schema format, the C<type> declared there is
  641: used as the authoritative output type and takes precedence over all
  642: heuristic code analysis:
  643: 
  644:     =head4 Output
  645: 
  646:         {
  647:             type => 'hashref',
  648:         }
  649: 
  650: This is the recommended way to document methods whose return type would
  651: otherwise be misidentified (e.g. a method that returns C<$self-E<gt>{cache}>
  652: where the cache happens to hold a hashref).
  653: 
  654: Using parentheses as the outer container emits C<type: array>, indicating a
  655: list-returning method.  L<App::Test::Generator> 0.39+ (with L<Test::Returns>
  656: 0.03+) captures these results in list context automatically:
  657: 
  658:     =head4 Output
  659: 
  660:         (
  661:             {
  662:                 type => 'hashref',
  663:             },
  664:             ...
  665:         )
  666: 
  667: =head3 List vs Scalar Context Detection
  668: 
  669: Automatically detects methods that return different values based on calling context:
  670: 
  671:     sub get_items {
  672:         my $self = $_[0];
  673:         return wantarray ? @items : scalar(@items);
  674:     }
  675: 
  676: Detection captures:
  677: 
  678: =over 4
  679: 
  680: =item * C<_context_aware> flag - Method uses wantarray
  681: 
  682: =item * C<_list_context> - Type returned in list context (e.g., 'array')
  683: 
  684: =item * C<_scalar_context> - Type returned in scalar context (e.g., 'integer')
  685: 
  686: =back
  687: 
  688: Recognizes both ternary operator patterns and conditional return patterns.
  689: 
  690: =head3 Void Context Methods
  691: 
  692: Identifies methods that don't return meaningful values:
  693: 
  694: =over 4
  695: 
  696: =item * Setters (C<set_*> methods)
  697: 
  698: =item * Mutators (C<add_*, remove_*, delete_*, clear_*, reset_*, update_*>)
  699: 
  700: =item * Loggers (C<log, debug, warn, error, info>)
  701: 
  702: =item * Methods with only empty returns
  703: 
  704: =back
  705: 
  706: Example:
  707: 
  708:     sub set_name {
  709:         my ($self, $name) = @_;
  710:         $self->{name} = $name;
  711:         return;  # Void context
  712:     }
  713: 
  714: Sets C<_void_context> flag and C<type =E<gt> 'void'>.
  715: 
  716: =head3 Method Chaining Detection
  717: 
  718: Identifies chainable methods that return C<$self> for fluent interfaces:
  719: 
  720:     sub set_width {
  721:         my ($self, $width) = @_;
  722:         $self->{width} = $width;
  723:         return $self;  # Chainable
  724:     }
  725: 
  726: Detection provides:
  727: 
  728: =over 4
  729: 
  730: =item * C<_returns_self> - Returns invocant for chaining
  731: 
  732: =item * C<class> - The class name being returned
  733: 
  734: =back
  735: 
  736: Also detects chaining documentation in POD (keywords: "chainable", "fluent interface",
  737: "returns self", "method chaining").
  738: 
  739: =head3 Error Return Conventions
  740: 
  741: Analyzes how methods signal errors:
  742: 
  743: B<Pattern Detection:>
  744: 
  745: =over 4
  746: 
  747: =item * C<undef_on_error> - Explicit C<return undef if/unless condition>
  748: 
  749: =item * C<implicit_undef> - Bare C<return if/unless condition>
  750: 
  751: =item * C<empty_list> - C<return ()> for list context errors
  752: 
  753: =item * C<zero_on_error> - Returns 0/false for boolean error indication
  754: 
  755: =item * C<exception_handling> - Uses eval blocks with error checking
  756: 
  757: =back
  758: 
  759: B<Example Analysis:>
  760: 
  761:     sub fetch_user {
  762:         my ($self, $id) = @_;
  763: 
  764:         return undef unless $id;        # undef_on_error
  765:         return undef if $id < 0;        # undef_on_error
  766: 
  767:         return $self->{users}{$id};
  768:     }
  769: 
  770: Results in:
  771: 
  772:     _error_return: 'undef'
  773:     _success_failure_pattern: 1
  774:     _error_handling: {
  775:         undef_on_error: ['$id', '$id < 0']
  776:     }
  777: 
  778: B<Success/Failure Pattern:>
  779: 
  780: Methods that return different types for success vs. failure are flagged with
  781: C<_success_failure_pattern>. Common patterns:
  782: 
  783: =over 4
  784: 
  785: =item * Returns value on success, undef on failure
  786: 
  787: =item * Returns true on success, false on failure
  788: 
  789: =item * Returns data on success, empty list on failure
  790: 
  791: =back
  792: 
  793: =head3 Success Indicator Detection
  794: 
  795: Methods that always return true (typically for side effects):
  796: 
  797:     sub update_status {
  798:         my ($self, $status) = @_;
  799:         $self->{status} = $status;
  800:         return 1;  # Success indicator
  801:     }
  802: 
  803: Sets C<_success_indicator> flag when method consistently returns 1.
  804: 
  805: =head3 Schema Output
  806: 
  807: Enhanced return analysis adds these fields to method schemas:
  808: 
  809:     output:
  810:       type: boolean              # Inferred return type
  811:       _context_aware: 1           # Uses wantarray
  812:       _list_context:
  813:         type: array
  814:       _scalar_context:
  815:         type: integer
  816:       _returns_self: 1               # Returns $self
  817:       _void_context: 1            # No meaningful return
  818:       _success_indicator: 1       # Always returns true
  819:       _error_return: undef        # How errors are signaled
  820:       _success_failure_pattern: 1 # Mixed return types
  821:       _error_handling:            # Detailed error patterns
  822:         undef_on_error: [...]
  823:         exception_handling: 1
  824: 
  825: This comprehensive analysis enables:
  826: 
  827: =over 4
  828: 
  829: =item * Better test generation (testing both contexts, error paths)
  830: 
  831: =item * Documentation generation (clear error conventions)
  832: 
  833: =item * API design validation (consistent error handling)
  834: 
  835: =item * Contract specification (precise return behavior)
  836: 
  837: =back
  838: 
  839: =head2 Example
  840: 
  841: For a method like:
  842: 
  843:     sub connect {
  844:         my ($self, $host, $port, $ssl, $file, $content) = @_;
  845: 
  846:         die if $file && $content;                    # mutually exclusive
  847:         die unless $host || $file;                   # required group
  848:         die "Port requires host" if $port && !$host; # dependency
  849:         die if $ssl && $port != 443;                 # value constraint
  850: 
  851:         # ... connection logic
  852:     }
  853: 
  854: The extractor generates:
  855: 
  856:     relationships:
  857:       - type: mutually_exclusive
  858:         params: [file, content]
  859:         description: Cannot specify both file and content
  860:       - type: required_group
  861:         params: [host, file]
  862:         logic: or
  863:         description: Must specify either host or file
  864:       - type: dependency
  865:         param: port
  866:         requires: host
  867:         description: port requires host to be specified
  868:       - type: value_constraint
  869:         if: ssl
  870:         then: port
  871:         operator: ==
  872:         value: 443
  873:         description: When ssl is specified, port must equal 443
  874: 
  875: =head1 MODERN PERL FEATURES
  876: 
  877: This module adds support for:
  878: 
  879: =head2 Subroutine Signatures (Perl 5.20+)
  880: 
  881:     sub connect($host, $port = 3306, %options) {
  882:         ...
  883:     }
  884: 
  885: Extracts: required params, optional params with defaults, slurpy params
  886: 
  887: =head2 Type Constraints (Perl 5.36+)
  888: 
  889:     sub calculate($x :Int, $y :Num) {
  890:         ...
  891:     }
  892: 
  893: Recognizes: Int, Num, Str, Bool, ArrayRef, HashRef, custom classes
  894: 
  895: =head3 Subroutine Attributes
  896: 
  897:     sub get_value :lvalue :Returns(Int) {
  898:         ...
  899:     }
  900: 
  901: Detects: :lvalue, :method, :Returns(Type), custom attributes
  902: 
  903: =head2 Postfix Dereferencing (Perl 5.20+)
  904: 
  905:     my @array = $arrayref->@*;
  906:     my %hash = $hashref->%*;
  907:     my @slice = $arrayref->@[1,3,5];
  908: 
  909: Tracks usage of modern dereferencing syntax
  910: 
  911: =head2 Field Declarations (Perl 5.38+)
  912: 
  913:     field $host :param = 'localhost';
  914:     field $port :param(port_number) = 3306;
  915:     field $logger :param :isa(Log::Any);
  916: 
  917: Extracts fields and maps them to parameters
  918: 
  919: =head2 Modern Perl Features Support
  920: 
  921: The schema extractor supports modern Perl syntax introduced in versions 5.20, 5.36, and 5.38+.
  922: 
  923: =head3 Subroutine Signatures (Perl 5.20+)
  924: 
  925: Automatically extracts parameters from native Perl signatures:
  926: 
  927:     use feature 'signatures';
  928: 
  929:     sub connect($host, $port = 3306, $database = undef) {
  930:         ...
  931:     }
  932: 
  933: Extracted schema includes:
  934: 
  935: =over 4
  936: 
  937: =item * Parameter positions
  938: 
  939: =item * Optional vs required parameters
  940: 
  941: =item * Default values from signature
  942: 
  943: =item * Slurpy parameters (@array, %hash)
  944: 
  945: =back
  946: 
  947: B<Example:>
  948: 
  949:     # Signature with defaults
  950:     sub process($file, %options) { ... }
  951: 
  952:     # Extracts:
  953:     # $file: position 0, required
  954:     # %options: position 1, optional, slurpy hash
  955: 
  956: =head3 Type Constraints in Signatures (Perl 5.36+)
  957: 
  958: Recognizes type constraints in signature parameters:
  959: 
  960:     sub calculate($x :Int, $y :Num, $name :Str = "result") {
  961:         return $x + $y;
  962:     }
  963: 
  964: Supported constraint types:
  965: 
  966: =over 4
  967: 
  968: =item * C<:Int, :Integer> -> integer
  969: 
  970: =item * C<:Num, :Number> -> number
  971: 
  972: =item * C<:Str, :String> -> string
  973: 
  974: =item * C<:Bool, :Boolean> -> boolean
  975: 
  976: =item * C<:ArrayRef, :Array> -> arrayref
  977: 
  978: =item * C<:HashRef, :Hash> -> hashref
  979: 
  980: =item * C<:ClassName> -> object with isa constraint
  981: 
  982: =back
  983: 
  984: Type constraints are combined with defaults when both are present.
  985: 
  986: =head3 Subroutine Attributes
  987: 
  988: Extracts and documents subroutine attributes:
  989: 
  990:     sub get_value :lvalue {
  991:         my $self = shift;
  992:         return $self->{value};
  993:     }
  994: 
  995:     sub calculate :Returns(Int) :method {
  996:         my ($self, $x, $y) = @_;
  997:         return $x + $y;
  998:     }
  999: 
 1000: Recognized attributes stored in C<_attributes> field:
 1001: 
 1002: =over 4
 1003: 
 1004: =item * C<:lvalue> - Method can be assigned to
 1005: 
 1006: =item * C<:method> - Explicitly marked as method
 1007: 
 1008: =item * C<:Returns(Type)> - Declares return type
 1009: 
 1010: =item * Custom attributes with values: C<:MyAttr(value)>
 1011: 
 1012: =back
 1013: 
 1014: =head3 Postfix Dereferencing (Perl 5.20+)
 1015: 
 1016: Detects usage of postfix dereferencing syntax:
 1017: 
 1018:     use feature 'postderef';
 1019: 
 1020:     sub process_array {
 1021:         my ($self, $arrayref) = @_;
 1022:         my @array = $arrayref->@*;        # Array dereference
 1023:         my @slice = $arrayref->@[1,3,5];  # Array slice
 1024:         return @array;
 1025:     }
 1026: 
 1027:     sub process_hash {
 1028:         my ($self, $hashref) = @_;
 1029:         my %hash = $hashref->%*;          # Hash dereference
 1030:         return keys %hash;
 1031:     }
 1032: 
 1033: Tracked features stored in C<_modern_features>:
 1034: 
 1035: =over 4
 1036: 
 1037: =item * C<array_deref> - Uses C<-E<gt>@*>
 1038: 
 1039: =item * C<hash_deref> - Uses C<-E<gt>%*>
 1040: 
 1041: =item * C<scalar_deref> - Uses C<-E<gt>$*>
 1042: 
 1043: =item * C<code_deref> - Uses C<-E<gt>&*>
 1044: 
 1045: =item * C<array_slice> - Uses C<-E<gt>@[...]>
 1046: 
 1047: =item * C<hash_slice> - Uses C<-E<gt>%{...}>
 1048: 
 1049: =back
 1050: 
 1051: =head3 Field Declarations (Perl 5.38+)
 1052: 
 1053: Extracts field declarations from class syntax and maps them to method parameters:
 1054: 
 1055:     use feature 'class';
 1056: 
 1057:     class DatabaseConnection {
 1058:         field $host :param = 'localhost';
 1059:         field $port :param = 3306;
 1060:         field $username :param(user);
 1061:         field $password :param;
 1062:         field $logger :param :isa(Log::Any);
 1063: 
 1064:         method connect() {
 1065:             # Fields available as instance variables
 1066:         }
 1067:     }
 1068: 
 1069: Field attributes:
 1070: 
 1071: =over 4
 1072: 
 1073: =item * C<:param> - Field is a constructor parameter (uses field name)
 1074: 
 1075: =item * C<:param(name)> - Field maps to parameter with different name
 1076: 
 1077: =item * C<:isa(Class)> - Type constraint for the field
 1078: 
 1079: =item * Default values in field declarations
 1080: 
 1081: =back
 1082: 
 1083: Extracted schema includes both field information in C<_fields> and merged parameter
 1084: information in C<input>, allowing proper validation of class constructors.
 1085: 
 1086: =head3 Mixed Modern and Traditional Syntax
 1087: 
 1088: The extractor handles code that mixes modern and traditional syntax:
 1089: 
 1090:     sub modern($x, $y = 5) {
 1091:         # Modern signature with default
 1092:     }
 1093: 
 1094:     sub traditional {
 1095:         my ($self, $x, $y) = @_;
 1096:         $y //= 5;  # Traditional default in code
 1097:         # Both extract same parameter information
 1098:     }
 1099: 
 1100: Priority order for parameter information:
 1101: 
 1102: =over 4
 1103: 
 1104: =item 1. Signature declarations (highest priority)
 1105: 
 1106: =item 2. Field declarations (for class methods)
 1107: 
 1108: =item 3. POD documentation
 1109: 
 1110: =item 4. Code analysis (lowest priority)
 1111: 
 1112: =back
 1113: 
 1114: This ensures that explicit declarations in signatures take precedence over
 1115: inferred information from code analysis.
 1116: 
 1117: =head3 Backwards Compatibility
 1118: 
 1119: All modern Perl feature detection is optional and automatic:
 1120: 
 1121: =over 4
 1122: 
 1123: =item * Traditional C<sub> declarations continue to work
 1124: 
 1125: =item * Code without modern features extracts parameters as before
 1126: 
 1127: =item * Modern features are additive - they enhance rather than replace existing extraction
 1128: 
 1129: =item * Each parameter carries one C<_source> value -- C<validator>, C<input_spec>, C<pod>, C<code>, C<signature> or C<field> -- which the harness uses for its type-checking decisions
 1130: 
 1131: =item * Each constraint that can come from more than one place also carries its own provenance in C<_constraint_source>, a hashref keyed by C<min>, C<max>, C<memberof>, C<matches>, C<nomatch> and C<notmemberof>.  A minimum documented in POD and a maximum enforced by the code on the same field therefore stay separate decisions: the documented one is a hint for valid data, the enforced one is a check
 1132: 
 1133: =back
 1134: 
 1135: =head2 _yamltest_hints
 1136: 
 1137: Each method schema returned by L</extract_all> now optionally includes a
 1138: C<_yamltest_hints> key, which provides guidance for automated test generation
 1139: based on the code analysis.
 1140: 
 1141: This is intended to help L<App::Test::Generator> create meaningful tests,
 1142: including boundary and invalid input cases, without manually specifying them.
 1143: 
 1144: The structure is a hashref with the following keys:
 1145: 
 1146: =over 4
 1147: 
 1148: =item * boundary_values
 1149: 
 1150: An arrayref of numeric values that represent boundaries detected from
 1151: comparisons in the code. These are derived from literals in statements
 1152: like C<$x < 0> or C<$y >= 255>. The generator can use these to create
 1153: boundary tests.
 1154: 
 1155: Example:
 1156: 
 1157:     _yamltest_hints:
 1158:       boundary_values: [0, 1, 100, 255]
 1159: 
 1160: =item * invalid_inputs
 1161: 
 1162: An arrayref of values that are likely to be rejected by the method,
 1163: based on checks like C<defined>, empty strings, or numeric validations.
 1164: 
 1165: Example:
 1166: 
 1167:     _yamltest_hints:
 1168:       invalid_inputs: [undef, '', -1]
 1169: 
 1170: =item * equivalence_classes
 1171: 
 1172: An arrayref intended to capture detected equivalence classes or patterns
 1173: among inputs. Currently this is empty by default, but future enhancements
 1174: may populate it based on detected input groupings.
 1175: 
 1176: Example:
 1177: 
 1178:     _yamltest_hints:
 1179:       equivalence_classes: []
 1180: 
 1181: =back
 1182: 
 1183: =head3 Usage
 1184: 
 1185: When calling C<extract_all>, each method schema will include
 1186: C<_yamltest_hints> if any hints were detected:
 1187: 
 1188:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all;
 1189:     my $hints  = $schemas->{example_method}->{_yamltest_hints};
 1190: 
 1191: You can then feed these hints into automated test generators to produce
 1192: negative tests, boundary tests, and parameter-specific test cases.
 1193: 
 1194: =head3 Notes
 1195: 
 1196: =over 4
 1197: 
 1198: =item * Hints are inferred heuristically from code and validation statements.
 1199: 
 1200: =item * Not all inputs are guaranteed to be detected; the feature is additive
 1201: and will never remove information from the schema.
 1202: 
 1203: =item * Currently, equivalence classes are not populated, but the field exists
 1204: for future extension.
 1205: 
 1206: =item * Boundary and invalid input hints are deduplicated to avoid repeated
 1207: test values.
 1208: 
 1209: =back
 1210: 
 1211: =head3 Examples
 1212: 
 1213: Given a method like:
 1214: 
 1215:     sub example {
 1216:         my ($x) = @_;
 1217:         die "negative" if $x < 0;
 1218:         return unless defined($x);
 1219:         return $x * 2;
 1220:     }
 1221: 
 1222: After running:
 1223: 
 1224:     my $extractor = App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor->new(
 1225:         input_file => 'TestHints.pm',
 1226:         output_dir => '/tmp',
 1227:         quiet    => 1,
 1228:     );
 1229: 
 1230:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all;
 1231: 
 1232: The schema for the method "example" will include:
 1233: 
 1234:     $schemas->{example} = {
 1235:         function => 'example',
 1236:         _confidence => {
 1237:             input  => 'unknown',
 1238:             output => 'unknown',
 1239:         },
 1240:         input => {
 1241:             x => {
 1242:                 type     => 'scalar',
 1243:                 optional => 0,
 1244:             }
 1245:         },
 1246:         output => {
 1247:             type => 'scalar',
 1248:         },
 1249:         _yamltest_hints => {
 1250:             boundary_values => [0, 1],
 1251:             invalid_inputs  => [undef, -1],
 1252:             equivalence_classes => [],
 1253:         },
 1254:         _notes => '...',
 1255:         _analysis => {
 1256:             input_confidence  => 'low',
 1257:             output_confidence => 'unknown',
 1258:             confidence_factors => {
 1259:                 input  => {...},
 1260:                 output => {...},
 1261:             },
 1262:             overall_confidence => 'low',
 1263:         },
 1264:         _fields => {},
 1265:         _modern_features => {},
 1266:         _attributes => {},
 1267:     };
 1268: 
 1269: =head1 METHODS
 1270: 
 1271: =head2 new
 1272: 
 1273: Construct a new SchemaExtractor for a given Perl source file.
 1274: 
 1275:     my $extractor = App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor->new(
 1276:         input_file           => 'lib/MyModule.pm',  # Required
 1277:         output_dir           => 'schemas/',         # Optional - only needed if writing schemas
 1278:         verbose              => 1,                  # Default: 0
 1279:         include_private      => 1,                  # Default: 0
 1280:         max_parameters       => 50,                 # Default: 20
 1281:         confidence_threshold => 0.7,               # Default: 0.5
 1282:         strict_pod           => 0|1|2,              # Default: 0 (off)
 1283:         allow_signature_exec => 1,                  # Default: 0 (off)
 1284:     );
 1285: 
 1286: =head3 Arguments
 1287: 
 1288: =over 4
 1289: 
 1290: =item * C<$input_file>
 1291: 
 1292: Path to the Perl source file to analyse. Required. Must exist on disk.
 1293: 
 1294: =item * C<output_dir>
 1295: 
 1296: Directory to write generated schema YAML files. Optional - only
 1297: required if C<_write_schema> will be called. Callers passing
 1298: C<no_write =E<gt> 1> to C<extract_all> do not need to supply it.
 1299: 
 1300: =item * C<verbose>
 1301: 
 1302: Print progress messages to stdout during analysis. Optional, default 0.
 1303: 
 1304: =item * C<include_private>
 1305: 
 1306: Include methods whose names begin with C<_> in the analysis. Optional,
 1307: default 0. Methods whose name begins with C<_new>, C<_init>, or
 1308: C<_build> are always included regardless of this setting (a prefix
 1309: match, so e.g. C<_build_attribute> and C<_init_logger> qualify too,
 1310: matching common Moose builder/initializer naming conventions).
 1311: 
 1312: =item * C<max_parameters>
 1313: 
 1314: Safety limit on the number of parameters analysed per method to prevent
 1315: runaway processing on pathological code. Optional, default 20.
 1316: 
 1317: =item * C<confidence_threshold>
 1318: 
 1319: Minimum confidence score (0.0-1.0) below which a schema is marked with
 1320: C<_low_confidence =E<gt> 1>. Optional, default 0.5.
 1321: 
 1322: =item * C<strict_pod>
 1323: 
 1324: Controls POD/code agreement validation. C<0> disables validation,
 1325: C<1> emits warnings, C<2> croaks on first disagreement. Also accepts
 1326: the strings C<off>, C<warn>, and C<fatal>. Optional, default 0.
 1327: 
 1328: =item * C<allow_signature_exec>
 1329: 
 1330: Opt-in flag allowing extraction of parameter types from a
 1331: L<Type::Params> C<signature_for()> declaration. This requires actually
 1332: running the C<signature_for> expression (sliced from the target
 1333: module's own source) in a forked C<perl -T> process, since
 1334: L<Type::Params> types are runtime objects that cannot be introspected
 1335: statically. Every other extraction path in this module is static
 1336: (L<PPI>-only) analysis that never executes any of the target module's
 1337: code; this is the one exception. Optional, default 0 (the
 1338: C<signature_for> path is silently skipped, with a warning under
 1339: C<verbose>, when off). Only enable this for modules whose code you
 1340: already trust enough to execute.
 1341: 
 1342: =back
 1343: 
 1344: =head3 Returns
 1345: 
 1346: A blessed hashref. Croaks if C<input_file> is missing or does not
 1347: exist on disk.
 1348: 
 1349: =head3 Side effects
 1350: 
 1351: Reads and parses the input file using L<PPI> at construction time.
 1352: 
 1353: =head3 API specification
 1354: 
 1355: =head4 input
 1356: 
 1357:     {
 1358:         input_file           => { type => SCALAR },

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 1359:         output_dir           => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1360:         verbose              => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1361:         include_private      => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1362:         max_parameters       => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1363:         confidence_threshold => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1364:         strict_pod           => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1365:         allow_signature_exec => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1366:     }

 1405: 
 1406: =head2 extract_all
 1407: 
 1408: Extract schemas for all qualifying methods in the module and return
 1409: them as a hashref.
 1410: 
 1411:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all();
 1412: 
 1413:     # Suppress writing .yml files to disk
 1414:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all(no_write => 1);
 1415: 
 1416: =head3 Arguments
 1417: 
 1418: =over 4
 1419: 
 1420: =item * C<no_write>
 1421: 
 1422: When true, schema files are not written to C<output_dir>. The returned
 1423: hashref is still fully populated. Useful when the caller wants to
 1424: inspect or augment schemas before deciding whether to write them.
 1425: Optional, default 0.
 1426: 
 1427: =back
 1428: 
 1429: =head3 Returns
 1430: 
 1431: A hashref mapping method name strings to schema hashrefs. Each schema
 1432: contains at minimum the keys C<function>, C<module>, C<input>,
 1433: C<output>, and C<_analysis>. See L</Generated Schema Structure> for
 1434: the full structure.
 1435: 
 1436: =head3 Side effects
 1437: 
 1438: Parses the input file with L<PPI>. Writes one YAML file per method to
 1439: C<output_dir> unless C<no_write> is set. Creates C<output_dir> if it
 1440: does not exist and writing is enabled.
 1441: 
 1442: =head3 Notes
 1443: 
 1444: Private methods (names beginning with C<_>) are excluded unless
 1445: C<include_private =E<gt> 1> was passed to C<new>. Duplicate method
 1446: names are deduplicated with a warning logged to stdout in verbose mode.
 1447: 
 1448: POD/code agreement validation is applied if C<strict_pod> was set in
 1449: C<new>. At level 2 (fatal), the first disagreement causes an immediate
 1450: croak.
 1451: 
 1452: =head3 API specification
 1453: 
 1454: =head4 input
 1455: 
 1456:     {
 1457:         self     => { type => OBJECT, isa => 'App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor' },
 1458:         no_write => { type => SCALAR, optional => 1 },
 1459:     }
 1460: 
 1461: =head4 output
 1462: 
 1463:     {
 1464:         type => HASHREF,
 1465:         keys => {
 1466:             '*' => {
 1467:                 type => HASHREF,
 1468:                 keys => {
 1469:                     function  => { type => SCALAR  },
 1470:                     module    => { type => SCALAR  },
 1471:                     input     => { type => HASHREF },
 1472:                     output    => { type => HASHREF },

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1473:                     _analysis => { type => HASHREF },
 1474:                 },
 1475:             },
 1476:         },
 1477:     }
 1478: 
 1479: =cut
 1480:

 4654: #
 4655: # Purpose:    Remove quoted strings and regex literals from a code fragment
 4656: #             before looking for arithmetic operators in it.  A '-' in
 4657: #             'Period-over-period', a '%' in sprintf '%04d', or a '/' in
 4658: #             /[0-9%]/ is text, not arithmetic, and reading it as an operator
 4659: #             typed string arguments and string-returning routines as numbers.
 4660: #
 4661: # Entry:      $code - source fragment (a parameter body or a return value).
 4662: #
 4663: # Exit:       The fragment with each quoted string and regex literal body
 4664: #             emptied.  Undef is treated as an empty fragment.
 4665: # --------------------------------------------------
 4666: sub _strip_literals {
 4667: 	my ($code) = @_;
 4668: 
 4669: 	return '' unless defined($code);
 4670: 
 4671: 	$code =~ s/'(?:\\.|[^'\\])*'/''/g;
 4672: 	$code =~ s/"(?:\\.|[^"\\])*"/""/g;
 4673: 
 4674: 	# m{...}, qr{...} and s{...}{...}, which a '/' pattern would miss.
 4675: 	$code =~ s{\b(?:qr|m|s|tr|y)\s*\{(?:\\.|[^{}\\]|\{[^{}]*\})*\}

 4679: 
 4680: 	return $code;
 4681: }

 8245: # --------------------------------------------------
 8246: # _positional_pod_input
 8247: #
 8248: # Purpose:    Recognise a lone documented input parameter that is really the
 8249: #             first positional argument under another name.
 8250: #
 8251: # Entry:      $pod        - parameters from _analyze_pod; mutated.
 8252: #             $code_params - parameters from code analysis; mutated.
 8253: #             $code       - the subroutine body as source text.
 8254: #
 8255: # Exit:       Returns the documented name when it is treated as position 0,
 8256: #             otherwise undef.
 8257: #
 8258: # Notes:      Two or more documented names describe a hash of arguments, and
 8259: #             a body that reads an argument hash (my %args = @_, $args->{...},
 8260: #             $_[0]{...}) keeps the named style even with one name.  A body
 8261: #             that shows no positional use at all -- a wrapper forwarding @_,
 8262: #             say -- proves nothing either way, so it stays named rather than
 8263: #             guessing.
 8264: #
 8265: # Side effects: On success sets position 0, drops stray documented names and
 8266: #             replaces the code analysis with the one argument, renamed.
 8267: # --------------------------------------------------
 8268: sub _positional_pod_input {
 8269: 	my ($self, $pod, $code_params, $code) = @_;
 8270: 
 8271: 	my @documented = grep { ($pod->{$_}{_source} // '') eq SOURCE_INPUT_SPEC } keys %$pod;
 8272: 	return undef unless @documented == 1;
 8273: 	return undef unless $self->_argument_style($code) eq 'positional';
 8274: 
 8275: 	my ($name) = @documented;
 8276: 	$pod->{$name}{position} = 0;
 8277: 
 8278: 	# The body names the same argument however it likes, so carry its facts
 8279: 	# (a directory tested with -d, say) to the documented name.
 8280: 	my @code_names = keys %$code_params;
 8281: 	my $source = $code_params->{$name} ? $name
 8282: 		: (@code_names == 1 ? $code_names[0] : undef);
 8283: 
 8284: 	# Empty list, not {}: a hashref here would become a one-element list and

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 8285: 	# land in the hash as a "HASH(0x...)" key.
 8286: 	%$code_params = $source ? ($name => $code_params->{$source}) : ();
 8287: 	%$pod = ($name => $pod->{$name});
 8288: 	$self->_log("  POD: single Input spec '$name' describes a positional argument");
 8289: 
 8290: 	return $name;
 8291: }

10398: # --------------------------------------------------
10399: # _detect_factory_method
10400: #
10401: # Purpose:    Detect whether a method is a factory
10402: #             that creates and returns object
10403: #             instances rather than operating on
10404: #             an existing instance.
10405: #
10406: # Entry:      $method_name     - method name string.
10407: #             $method_body     - method source string.
10408: #             $current_package - current package name.
10409: #             $method_info     - method hashref
10410: #                                (optional).
10411: #
10412: # Exit:       Returns a factory_info hashref on
10413: #             detection, or undef if the method
10414: #             is not a factory.
10415: #             The hashref includes: returns_class,
10416: #             confidence, and one of:
10417: #             returns_blessed, returns_new,
10418: #             returns_factory_result, pod_hint.
10419: #
10420: # Side effects: None.
10421: # --------------------------------------------------
10422: sub _detect_factory_method {
●10423 → 10428 → 1043310423: 	my ($self, $method_name, $method_body, $current_package, $method_info) = @_;
10424: 
10425: 	my %factory_info;
10426: 
10427: 	# Check method name patterns
10428: 	if ($method_name =~ /^(create_|make_|build_|get_)/i) {
10429: 		$factory_info{name_pattern} = 1;
10430: 	}
10431: 
10432: 	# Look for object creation patterns in the method body
●10433 → 10433 → 1048410433: 	if ($method_body) {
10434: 		# Pattern 1: Returns a blessed reference
10435: 		if ($method_body =~ /return\s+bless\s*\{[^}]*\},\s*['"]?(\w+(?:::\w+)*|\$\w+)['"]?/s ||
10436: 			$method_body =~ /bless\s*\{[^}]*\},\s*['"]?(\w+(?:::\w+)*|\$\w+)['"]?.*return/s) {
10437: 			my $class_name = $1;
10438: 
10439: 			# Handle variable class names
10440: 			if ($class_name =~ /^\$(class|self|package)$/) {
10441: 				$factory_info{returns_class} = $current_package;
10442: 			} elsif ($class_name =~ /^\$/) {
10443: 				$factory_info{returns_class} = 'VARIABLE';	# Unknown variable
10444: 			} else {
10445: 				$factory_info{returns_class} = $class_name;
10446: 			}
10447: 
10448: 			$factory_info{returns_blessed} = 1;
10449: 			$factory_info{confidence} = 'high';
10450: 			return \%factory_info;
10451: 		}
10452: 
10453: 		# Pattern 2: Returns ->new() call on class or $self
10454: 		if ($method_body =~ /return\s+([\$\w:]+)->new\(/s ||
10455: 			$method_body =~ /([\$\w:]+)->new\(.*return/s) {
10456: 			my $target = $1;
10457: 
10458: 			# Determine what class is being instantiated
10459: 			if ($target eq '$self' || $target eq 'shift' || $target =~ /^\$/) {

10461: 				$factory_info{self_new} = 1;
10462: 			} elsif ($target =~ /::/) {
10463: 				$factory_info{returns_class} = $target;
10464: 				$factory_info{external_class} = 1;
10465: 			} else {
10466: 				$factory_info{returns_class} = $target;
10467: 			}
10468: 
10469: 			$factory_info{returns_new} = 1;
10470: 			$factory_info{confidence} = 'medium';
10471: 			return \%factory_info;
10472: 		}
10473: 
10474: 		# Pattern 3: Returns an object from another factory method
10475: 		if ($method_body =~ /return\s+([\$\w:]+)->(create_|make_|build_|get_)/i ||
10476: 			$method_body =~ /([\$\w:]+)->(create_|make_|build_|get_).*return/si) {
10477: 			$factory_info{returns_factory_result} = 1;
10478: 			$factory_info{confidence} = 'low';
10479: 			return \%factory_info;
10480: 		}
10481: 	}
10482: 
10483: 	# Check for return type hints in POD if available
●10484 → 10484 → 1049310484: 	if ($method_info && ref($method_info) eq 'HASH' && $method_info->{pod}) {
10485: 		my $pod = $method_info->{pod};
10486: 		if ($pod =~ /returns?\s+(?:an?\s+)?(object|instance|new\s+\w+)/i) {
10487: 			$factory_info{pod_hint} = 1;
10488: 			$factory_info{confidence} = 'low';
10489: 			return \%factory_info;
10490: 		}
10491: 	}
10492: 
10493: 	return undef;
10494: }

10496: # --------------------------------------------------
10497: # _detect_singleton_pattern
10498: #
10499: # Purpose:    Detect singleton accessor methods
10500: #             that return a shared instance rather
10501: #             than creating a new object, by
10502: #             checking the method name and body
10503: #             for singleton patterns.
10504: #
10505: # Entry:      $method_name - method name string.
10506: #             $method_body - method source string.
10507: #
10508: # Exit:       Returns a singleton_info hashref on
10509: #             detection (always contains at least
10510: #             name_pattern => 1), or undef if the
10511: #             method name does not match the
10512: #             singleton accessor pattern.
10513: #
10514: # Side effects: None.
10515: #
10516: # Notes:      Only fires for methods named
10517: #             instance, get_instance, singleton,
10518: #             or shared_instance. Methods not
10519: #             matching these names always return
10520: #             undef regardless of body content.
10521: # --------------------------------------------------
10522: sub _detect_singleton_pattern {
●10523 → 10533 → 1056210523: 	my ($self, $method_name, $method_body) = @_;
10524: 
10525: 	# Check method name patterns
10526: 	return undef unless $method_name =~ /^(instance|get_instance|singleton|shared_instance)$/i;
10527: 
10528: 	my %singleton_info = (
10529: 		name_pattern => 1,
10530: 	);
10531: 
10532: 	# Look for singleton patterns in code
10533: 	if ($method_body) {
10534: 		# Pattern 1: Static/state variable holding instance
10535: 		if ($method_body =~ /(?:my\s+)?(?:our\s+)?\$(?:instance|_instance|singleton)\b/s ||
10536: 			$method_body =~ /state\s+\$(?:instance|_instance|singleton)\b/s) {
10537: 			$singleton_info{static_variable} = 1;
10538: 			$singleton_info{confidence} = 'high';
10539: 		}
10540: 
10541: 		# Pattern 2: Returns $instance if defined (with better regex)
10542: 		if ($method_body =~ /return\s+\$instance\s+if\s+(?:defined\s+)?\$instance/ ||
10543: 			$method_body =~ /unless\s+\$instance.*?=\s*.*?new/) {
10544: 			$singleton_info{returns_instance} = 1;
10545: 			$singleton_info{confidence} = 'high';
10546: 		}
10547: 
10548: 		# Pattern 3: ||= new() pattern (with better regex)
10549: 		if ($method_body =~ /\$instance\s*\|\|=\s*.*?new/ ||
10550: 			$method_body =~ /\$instance\s*=\s*.*?new\s+unless\s+(?:defined\s+)?\$instance/) {
10551: 			$singleton_info{lazy_initialization} = 1;
10552: 			$singleton_info{confidence} = 'medium';
10553: 		}
10554: 
10555: 		# Pattern 4: Direct return of $instance variable
10556: 		if ($method_body =~ /return\s+\$instance;/) {
10557: 			$singleton_info{returns_instance} = 1;
10558: 			$singleton_info{confidence} = 'high' unless $singleton_info{confidence};
10559: 		}
10560: 	}
10561: 
10562: 	return \%singleton_info if keys %singleton_info > 0;	# Need at least name pattern
10563: 
10564: 	return undef;
10565: }

10567: # --------------------------------------------------
10568: # _detect_instance_method
10569: #
10570: # Purpose:    Detect whether a method is an
10571: #             instance method that requires a
10572: #             blessed object ($self) to be called,
10573: #             through multiple detection patterns
10574: #             of varying confidence.
10575: #
10576: # Entry:      $method_name - method name string.
10577: #             $method_body - method source string.
10578: #
10579: # Exit:       Returns an instance_info hashref if
10580: #             any instance method signal is found.
10581: #             Returns undef if no signals are
10582: #             detected.
10583: #             The hashref may contain: explicit_self,
10584: #             shift_self, uses_self,
10585: #             accesses_object_data,
10586: #             calls_instance_methods,
10587: #             private_method, and confidence.
10588: #
10589: # Side effects: None.
10590: # --------------------------------------------------
10591: sub _detect_instance_method {
●10592 → 10597 → 1062210592: 	my ($self, $method_name, $method_body) = @_;
10593: 
10594: 	my %instance_info;
10595: 
10596: 	# Pattern 1: my ($self, ...) = @_;
10597: 	if ($method_body =~ /my\s*\(\s*\$self\s*[,)]/) {
10598: 		$instance_info{explicit_self} = 1;
10599: 		$instance_info{confidence} = 'high';
10600: 	}
10601: 
10602: 	# Pattern 1b: my $self = $_[0];  (direct-index style)
10603: 	elsif ($method_body =~ /my\s+\$self\s*=\s*\$_\[0\]/) {
10604: 		$instance_info{explicit_self} = 1;
10605: 		$instance_info{confidence} = 'high';
10606: 	}
10607: 
10608: 	# Pattern 2: my $self = shift;
10609: 	elsif ($method_body =~ /my\s+\$self\s*=\s*shift/) {
10610: 		$instance_info{shift_self} = 1;
10611: 		$instance_info{confidence} = 'high';
10612: 	}
10613: 
10614: 	# Pattern 3: Uses $self->something (including hash/array access)
10615: 	# This catches $self->{value} and $self->[0] as well as $self->method()
10616: 	elsif ($method_body =~ /\$self\s*->\s*(\w+|[\{\[])/) {
10617: 		$instance_info{uses_self} = 1;
10618: 		$instance_info{confidence} = 'medium';
10619: 	}
10620: 
10621: 	# Pattern 4: Accesses object data: $self->{...}, $self->[...]
●10622 → 10622 → 1062810622: 	if ($method_body =~ /\$self\s*->\s*[\{\[]/) {
10623: 		$instance_info{accesses_object_data} = 1;
10624: 		$instance_info{confidence} = 'high' unless $instance_info{confidence} eq 'high';
10625: 	}
10626: 
10627: 	# Pattern 5: Calls other instance methods on $self
●10628 → 10628 → 1063710628: 	if ($method_body =~ /\$self\s*->\s*(\w+)\s*\(/s) {
10629: 		$instance_info{calls_instance_methods} = [];
10630: 		while ($method_body =~ /\$self\s*->\s*(\w+)\s*\(/g) {
10631: 			push @{$instance_info{calls_instance_methods}}, $1;
10632: 		}
10633: 		$instance_info{confidence} = 'high' if @{$instance_info{calls_instance_methods}};
10634: 	}
10635: 
10636: 	# Pattern 6: Method name suggests instance method (not perfect but helpful)
●10637 → 10637 → 1064310637: 	if ($method_name =~ /^_/ && $method_name !~ /^_new/) {
10638: 		# Private methods are usually instance methods
10639: 		$instance_info{private_method} = 1;
10640: 		$instance_info{confidence} = 'low' unless exists $instance_info{confidence};
10641: 	}
10642: 
10643: 	return \%instance_info if keys %instance_info;
10644: 	return undef;
10645: }

10648: # _check_inheritance_for_constructor
10649: #
10650: # Purpose:    Determine whether the current package
10651: #             uses an inherited constructor from a
10652: #             parent class, by examining use parent,
10653: #             use base, and @ISA declarations.
10654: #
10655: # Entry:      $current_package - current package
10656: #                                name string.
10657: #             $method_body     - method source string
10658: #                                (checked for SUPER::
10659: #                                calls).
10660: #
10661: # Exit:       Returns an inheritance_info hashref
10662: #             if any inheritance information is
10663: #             found, or undef otherwise.
10664: #             The hashref may contain:
10665: #             parent_statements, isa_array,
10666: #             uses_super, calls_super_new,
10667: #             has_own_constructor,
10668: #             use_parent_constructor, parent_class.
10669: #
10670: # Side effects: None.
10671: # --------------------------------------------------
10672: sub _check_inheritance_for_constructor {
●10673 → 10685 → 1070110673: 	my ($self, $current_package, $method_body) = @_;
10674: 
10675: 	my $doc = $self->{_document};
10676: 	return undef unless $doc;
10677: 
10678: 	my %inheritance_info;
10679: 
10680: 	# 1. Look for parent/base statements
10681: 	my @parent_classes;

10684: 	my $includes = $doc->find('PPI::Statement::Include') || [];

10686: 		my $content = $inc->content;
10687: 		if ($content =~ /use\s+(parent|base)\s+['"]?([\w:]+)['"]?/) {
10688: 			push @parent_classes, $2;
10689: 			$inheritance_info{parent_statements} = \@parent_classes;
10690: 		}
10691: 		# Also check for multiple parents: use parent qw(Class1 Class2)
10692: 		if ($content =~ /use\s+(parent|base)\s+qw?[\(\[]?(.+?)[\)\]]?;/) {
10693: 			my $parents = $2;
10694: 			my @multi_parents = split /\s+/, $parents;
10695: 			push @parent_classes, @multi_parents;
10696: 			$inheritance_info{parent_statements} = \@parent_classes;
10697: 		}
10698: 	}

10751: # --------------------------------------------------
10752: # _detect_constructor_requirements
10753: #
10754: # Purpose:    Analyse the new() method of the
10755: #             current or target package to determine
10756: #             what parameters the constructor
10757: #             requires, including required and
10758: #             optional parameters and their defaults.
10759: #
10760: # Entry:      $current_package - the package being
10761: #                                analysed.
10762: #             $target_package  - the package whose
10763: #                                constructor will

10765: #                                differ from current

10767: #                                constructors).

10770: #             success, or undef if no new() method

10775: # Side effects: None.

10777: sub _detect_constructor_requirements {

10789: 			note => "Constructor for external class $target_package - parameters unknown"

●10794 → 10807 → 1081810794: 	my $new_method = $doc->find_first(sub {

10826: 		$requirements{parameters} = \@shift_params;
10827: 		$requirements{parameter_count} = scalar @shift_params;
10828: 		$requirements{shift_pattern} = 1;
10829: 	}

●10832 → 10833 → 1083610832: 	my @required_params;

10834: 		push @required_params, $1;

●10836 → 10836 → 1084010836: 	if ($body =~ /die.*unless.*(?:defined\s+)?\$(\w+)/g) {
10837: 		push @required_params, $1;

10870: # --------------------------------------------------
10871: # _detect_external_object_dependency
10872: #
10873: # Purpose:    Detect whether a method creates or
10874: #             depends on objects from classes other
10875: #             than the current package, by scanning
10876: #             for ->new() calls on named classes
10877: #             and method calls on typed variables.
10878: #
10879: # Entry:      $method_body - method source string.
10880: #                            May be undef.
10881: #
10882: # Exit:       Returns a dependency_info hashref if
10883: #             external object usage is found, or
10884: #             undef otherwise.
10885: #             The hashref may contain:
10886: #             creates_objects (arrayref of class
10887: #             names), uses_objects (arrayref of
10888: #             class names), and package (the primary
10889: #             dependency class).
10890: #
10891: # Side effects: None.
10892: # --------------------------------------------------
10893: sub _detect_external_object_dependency {
●10894 → 10903 → 1090910894: 	my ($self, $method_body) = @_;
10895: 
10896: 	return undef unless $method_body;
10897: 
10898: 	my %dependency_info;
10899: 
10900: 	# Pattern 1: Creates objects of other classes with ->new() or ->create()
10901: 	# Reset pos for global match
10902: 	pos($method_body) = 0;
10903: 	while ($method_body =~ /(\w+(?:::\w+)*)->(?:new|create)\(/g) {
10904: 		my $class = $1;
10905: 		next if $class eq 'main' || $class eq '__PACKAGE__' || $class =~ /^\$/;
10906: 		push @{$dependency_info{creates_objects}}, $class;
10907: 	}
10908: 
●10909 → 10909 → 1091710909: 	if ($dependency_info{creates_objects}) {
10910: 		# Remove duplicates
10911: 		my %seen;
10912: 		$dependency_info{creates_objects} = [grep { !$seen{$_}++ } @{$dependency_info{creates_objects}}];
10913: 		$dependency_info{package} = $dependency_info{creates_objects}[0];
10914: 	}
10915: 
10916: 	# Pattern 2: Calls methods on objects from other classes
●10917 → 10917 → 1094710917: 	if ($method_body =~ /\$(\w+)->\w+\(/) {
10918: 		my %object_vars;
10919: 		# Reset pos for global match â€” the if check above used a
10920: 		# non-/g match so it cannot have advanced pos, but the while
10921: 		# loop's own /g matches still need to start from the beginning.
10922: 		pos($method_body) = 0;
10923: 		while ($method_body =~ /\$(\w+)->\w+\(/g) {
10924: 			$object_vars{$1}++;
10925: 		}
10926: 
10927: 		# Try to determine type of object variables
10928: 		my @object_classes;
10929: 		foreach my $var (keys %object_vars) {
10930: 			# Look for type declarations or assignments
10931: 			if ($method_body =~ /my\s+\$$var\s*=\s*(\w+(?:::\w+)+)->(?:new|create)/) {
10932: 				push @object_classes, $1;
10933: 			} elsif ($method_body =~ /my\s+\$$var\s*=\s*(\w+(?:::\w+)+)->/) {
10934: 				push @object_classes, $1;
10935: 			}
10936: 		}
10937: 
10938: 		if (@object_classes) {
10939: 			$dependency_info{uses_objects} = \@object_classes;
10940: 			$dependency_info{package} = $object_classes[0] unless $dependency_info{package};
10941: 		}
10942: 	}
10943: 
10944: 	# Pattern 3: Receives objects as parameters (type hints in comments/POD)
10945: 	# This would need integration with parameter analysis
10946: 
10947: 	return \%dependency_info if keys %dependency_info;
10948: 	return undef;
10949: }

10951: # --------------------------------------------------
10952: # _get_parent_class
10953: #
10954: # Purpose:    Find the first parent class of the
10955: #             current package by searching the
10956: #             PPI document for use parent, use base,
10957: #             or our @ISA declarations.
10958: #
10959: # Entry:      None (operates on $self->{_document}).
10960: #
10961: # Exit:       Returns the parent class name string,
10962: #             or undef if no parent is found.
10963: #
10964: # Side effects: None.
10965: # --------------------------------------------------
10966: sub _get_parent_class {
●10967 → 10979 → 1098510967: 	my $self = $_[0];
10968: 
10969: 	my $doc = $self->{_document};
10970: 	return unless $doc;
10971: 
10972: 	# Look for use parent statements
10973: 	my $parent_stmt = $doc->find_first(sub {
10974: 		$_[1]->isa('PPI::Statement::Include') &&
10975: 		$_[1]->type eq 'use' &&
10976: 		$_[1]->module =~ /^(parent|base)$/ &&
10977: 		$_[1]->arguments =~ /['"](\w+(?:::\w+)*)['"]/
10978: 	});
10979: 	if ($parent_stmt) {
10980: 		my $parent = $1;
10981: 		return $parent;
10982: 	}
10983: 
10984: 	# Look for @ISA assignment
●10985 → 10989 → 1099310985: 	my $isa_stmt = $doc->find_first(sub {
10986: 		$_[1]->isa('PPI::Statement') &&
10987: 		$_[1]->content =~ /our\s+\@ISA\s*=\s*\(\s*['"](\w+(?:::\w+)*)['"]\s*\)/
10988: 	});
10989: 	if ($isa_stmt && $isa_stmt->content =~ /['"](\w+(?:::\w+)*)['"]/) {
10990: 		return $1;
10991: 	}
10992: 
10993: 	return;
10994: }

10996: # --------------------------------------------------
10997: # _get_class_for_instance_method
10998: #
10999: # Purpose:    Determine which class should be used
11000: #             for object instantiation when testing
11001: #             an instance method, preferring the
11002: #             current package if it has a new()
11003: #             method, falling back to the parent
11004: #             class otherwise.
11005: #
11006: # Entry:      None (operates on $self->{_document}).
11007: #
11008: # Exit:       Returns the package name string to
11009: #             use for instantiation. Returns
11010: #             'UNKNOWN_PACKAGE' if no package
11011: #             statement is found.
11012: #
11013: # Side effects: Stores the package name in
11014: #               $self->{_package_name} if not
11015: #               already set.
11016: # --------------------------------------------------
11017: sub _get_class_for_instance_method {
●11018 → 11032 → 1103711018: 	my $self = $_[0];
11019: 
11020: 	# Get the current package
11021: 	my $doc = $self->{_document};
11022: 	my $package_stmt = $doc->find_first('PPI::Statement::Package');
11023: 	return 'UNKNOWN_PACKAGE' unless $package_stmt;
11024: 	my $package_name = $package_stmt->namespace;
11025: 	$self->{_package_name} //= $package_name;
11026: 
11027: 	# Check if the current package has a 'new' method
11028: 	my $has_new = $doc->find(sub {
11029: 		$_[1]->isa('PPI::Statement::Sub') && $_[1]->name eq 'new'
11030: 	});
11031: 
11032: 	if ($has_new) {
11033: 		return $package_name;
11034: 	}
11035: 
11036: 	# Otherwise, try to get the parent class
11037: 	my $parent = $self->_get_parent_class();
11038: 	return $parent if $parent;
11039: 
11040: 	# Fallback to current package
11041: 	return $package_name;
11042: }

11044: # --------------------------------------------------
11045: # _extract_default_value
11046: #
11047: # Purpose:    Extract a default value for a named
11048: #             parameter from a method body by
11049: #             matching multiple common Perl default
11050: #             assignment idioms.
11051: #
11052: # Entry:      $param - parameter name string.
11053: #             $code  - method body source string.
11054: #
11055: # Exit:       Returns the cleaned default value
11056: #             scalar on success, or undef if no
11057: #             default assignment pattern is found.
11058: #
11059: # Side effects: None.
11060: #
11061: # Notes:      Eight patterns are tried in order:
11062: #             ||, //=, defined ternary, unless
11063: #             defined, ||=, //, multi-line if
11064: #             !defined, unless defined block.
11065: #             Comment lines are stripped from the
11066: #             code before matching to avoid false
11067: #             positives. Delegates to
11068: #             _clean_default_value for value
11069: #             normalisation.
11070: # --------------------------------------------------
11071: sub _extract_default_value {
●11072 → 11084 → 1109211072: 	my ($self, $param, $code) = @_;
11073: 
11074: 	return undef unless $param && $code;
11075: 
11076: 	# Clean up the code for easier pattern matching
11077: 	# Remove comments to avoid false positives
11078: 	my $clean_code = $code;
11079: 	$clean_code =~ s/#.*$//gm;
11080: 	$clean_code =~ s/^\s+|\s+$//g;
11081: 
11082: 	# Pattern 1: $param = $param || 'default_value'
11083: 	# Also handles: $param = $arg || 'default'
11084: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*=\s*(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)\s*\|\|\s*([^;]+)/) {
11085: 		my $default = $1;
11086: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
11087: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
11088: 		return $default if defined $default;
11089: 	}
11090: 
11091: 	# Pattern 2: $param //= 'default_value'
●11092 → 11092 → 1110111092: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*\/\/=\s*([^;]+)/) {
11093: 		my $default = $1;
11094: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
11095: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
11096: 		return $default if defined $default;
11097: 	}
11098: 
11099: 	# Pattern 3: $param = defined $param ? $param : 'default'
11100: 	# Also handles: $param = defined $arg ? $arg : 'default'
●11101 → 11101 → 1110911101: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*=\s*defined\s+(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)\s*\?\s*(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)\s*:\s*([^;]+)/) {
11102: 		my $default = $1;
11103: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
11104: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
11105: 		return $default if defined $default;
11106: 	}
11107: 
11108: 	# Pattern 4: $param = 'default' unless defined $param;
●11109 → 11109 → 1111611109: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*=\s*([^;]+?)\s+unless\s+defined\s+(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)/) {
11110: 		my $default = $1;
11111: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
11112: 		return $default if defined $default;
11113: 	}
11114: 
11115: 	# Pattern 5: $param ||= 'default'
●11116 → 11116 → 1112411116: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*\|\|=\s*([^;]+)/) {
11117: 		my $default = $1;
11118: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
11119: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
11120: 		return $default if defined $default;
11121: 	}
11122: 
11123: 	# Pattern 6: $param = $arg // 'default'
●11124 → 11124 → 1113211124: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*=\s*(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)\s*\/\/\s*([^;]+)/) {
11125: 		my $default = $1;
11126: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
11127: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
11128: 		return $default if defined $default;
11129: 	}
11130: 
11131: 	# Pattern 7: Multi-line: if (!defined $param) { $param = 'default'; }
●11132 → 11132 → 1114011132: 	if ($clean_code =~ /if\s*\(\s*!defined\s+\$$param\s*\)\s*\{[^}]*\$$param\s*=\s*([^;]+)/s) {
11133: 		my $default = $1;
11134: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
11135: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
11136: 		return $default if defined $default;
11137: 	}

●11140 → 11140 → 1114711140: 	if ($clean_code =~ /unless\s*\(\s*defined\s+\$$param\s*\)\s*\{[^}]*\$$param\s*=\s*([^;]+)/s) {
11141: 		my $default = $1;
11142: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
11143: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
11144: 		return $default if defined $default;

11151: # _extract_test_hints
11152: #
11153: # Purpose:    Extract structured test hints from
11154: #             a method's code and schema, including
11155: #             boundary values, invalid inputs, and
11156: #             valid input examples from POD.
11157: #
11158: # Entry:      $method - method hashref.
11159: #             $schema - schema hashref as built so
11160: #                       far by _analyze_method.
11161: #
11162: # Exit:       Returns a hints hashref with keys:
11163: #             boundary_values, invalid_inputs,
11164: #             equivalence_classes, valid_inputs.
11165: #             Keys with empty arrays are deleted
11166: #             before returning.
11167: #
11168: # Side effects: None.
11169: # --------------------------------------------------
11170: sub _extract_test_hints {
●11171 → 11187 → 1119111171: 	my ($self, $method, $schema) = @_;
11172: 
11173: 	my %hints = (
11174: 		boundary_values => [],
11175: 		invalid_inputs => [],
11176: 		equivalence_classes => [],
11177: 		valid_inputs => [],
11178: 	);
11179:

11188: 		delete $hints{$k} unless @{$hints{$k}};
11189: 	}
11190: 
11191: 	return \%hints;
11192: }

11211: sub _extract_invalid_input_hints {

11231: # _extract_boundary_value_hints
11232: #
11233: # Purpose:    Extract numeric boundary values from
11234: #             comparison operators in a method body,
11235: #             adding both the boundary value and
11236: #             the value one step either side.
11237: #

11239: #             $hints - hints hashref (modified in
11240: #                      place via boundary_values key).
11241: #
11242: # Exit:       Returns nothing. Appends to and
11243: #             deduplicates $hints->{boundary_values}.
11244: #
11245: # Side effects: None.
11246: # --------------------------------------------------
11247: sub _extract_boundary_value_hints {
●11248 → 11250 → 1126511248: 	my ($self, $code, $hints) = @_;
11249:

11251: 		my ($op, $n) = ($1, $2);
11252: 
11253: 		if ($op eq '<') {

11257: 		} elsif ($op eq '>') {

11259: 		} elsif ($op eq '>=') {

11265: 	my %seen;
11266: 	$hints->{boundary_values} = [ grep { !$seen{$_}++ } @{ $hints->{boundary_values} } ];
